# The known population of 1 July 1835

Generated by `tools/profile_population_1835.py --build`. Every figure is re-derived; `--check` proves this file is what the layer says. Ticket T-1160, as of 2026-09-18.

> A PROFILE OF THE EVIDENCE, NOT OF THE TOWN. 1282 person entries in 1258 households against a town census of 3,265 people. What is thin below is the record, and the tickets named against each hole are what fill it.

## Headcount

1282 person entries in 1258 households. The town census of November 1835 counts 3,265 people in 398 dwellings, so this layer is the part of the town the sources name and no more.

*No PERSON carries the `reconstructed` grade (0) and this ticket does not move that: reconstruction begins at T-1167 under its own programme. But 3669 ATTRIBUTE values already do, and a person-grade count hides them — which is the whole reason T-1158 puts a tier on the field rather than on the card.*

## Sex

689 of 1282 persons (53.7%) can be sexed at all — 689 because a source records it, 0 from a gendered title, 0 from a forename that stands in exactly one sex's period pool. The rest are an initial, a surname alone, or a forename both sexes used.

### Sex by the rule that says so

| sex | persons | share | recorded | inferred_title | inferred_forename | unknown |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| male | 647 | 50.5% | 647 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| female | 42 | 3.3% | 42 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| unknown | 593 | 46.3% | 0 | 0 | 0 | 593 |

### The rules themselves

| rule | persons | share | what it reads |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| recorded | 689 | 53.7% | a source records the sex on the card |
| inferred_title | 0 | 0.0% | the read name carries a gendered title (Mrs, Miss, Widow, Mr) |
| inferred_forename | 0 | 0.0% | the forename stands in exactly one sex's period pool and in no other |
| unknown | 593 | 46.3% | the name is an initial, a surname alone, or carries neither signal |

*A rank or a professional style — Capt., Rev., Dr. — is NOT read as male here. In this town it would almost always be right and it would still be an inference about the period rather than about the person; the age axis takes the honest half of that reading instead.*

## Age

64 of 1282 persons carry a year or an age; 419 more are placed in an adult band by what they are recorded DOING — a poll, a tax list, a muster, a trade, an office, a marriage. 799 persons carry nothing that bears on age. THERE IS NO AGE PYRAMID HERE: 64 dated ages cannot make one, which is T-1174's brief.

### Age bands

| band | persons | share | what it means |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| birth_year_known | 64 | 5.0% | a source gives the year, however loosely |
| age_stated | 0 | 0.0% | a source gives an age at 1 July 1835 |
| adult_by_civic_list | 192 | 15.0% | an adult: a voter, a taxpayer or a man of militia age |
| adult_by_role | 212 | 16.5% | an adult: a trade, profession or office is not held by a child |
| adult_by_marriage_or_parenthood | 15 | 1.2% | an adult on the register's own face |
| child_by_baptism | 0 | 0.0% | a child |
| unknown | 799 | 62.3% | nothing on the card bears on age at all |

## Origin and arrival

Every household carries an arrival block and 95.1% of them (1196) hold a `not_later_than` BOUND rather than an arrival: somebody was writing to that name at Chicago by a date, and nothing says when they came. Only 1258 households name an origin and 1258 a reason for coming.

| axis | households | share | attested | inferred | reconstructed | unknown |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| origin (state or country) | 1258 | 100.0% | 11 | 32 | 1215 | 0 |
| arrival date or bound | 1258 | 100.0% | 52 | 1200 | 6 | 0 |
| reason for coming | 1258 | 100.0% | 12 | 13 | 1233 | 0 |

| The Peck household | trade_or_forwarding | To open a store with a shipped stock of goods | attested |
| The Sabine household — a documented forwarding and commission, unplaced in the town | trade_or_forwarding | The carrying trade at the head of the lake | reconstructed |

*`canal_and_harbour_works` and `land_purchase` are both 0. The harbour works were the largest employer in the town in 1835 and the land sales were why much of the transient population was here; no household in the known layer states either as its reason. That silence is T-1178's brief, not a finding about the town.*

*NOBODY'S COMMUNITY IS READ OFF THEIR NAME HERE. A surname pool is how T-1177 will RECONSTRUCT the Irish, German, French-Canadian, Métis, Native and free Black town the register implies; it is not evidence about a person the sources merely name, and using it as evidence would manufacture exactly the attributions that ticket is required to mark as reconstructed.*

*What the sources are silent about, in this layer: every child, every servant and apprentice, the garrison below officer rank, the harbour-works gang, the crews in port, and the women of nearly every household the returns name.*

## What the town should have held

Every axis above is a count of the KNOWN layer. This is the list of what it is missing, by bucket and by the ticket that owns it. THERE ARE NO NUMBERS IN THIS SECTION ON PURPOSE: the quantities are T-1293's model and T-1166's order book, and a number typed here would be a second, unsourced answer to the same question.

### The buckets

| bucket | the ticket that owns it |
|---|---|
| the women and children of the households the returns name | T-1170, T-1171, T-1174 |
| servants, apprentices, journeymen and clerks | T-1171, T-1173, T-1189 |
| the labouring trades: labourers, carpenters, teamsters, sawyers, boatmen | T-1173 |
| boarders, lodgers and hotel guests, and the beds they filled | T-1175 |
| the Fort Dearborn garrison below officer rank, and soldiers' families | T-1176 |
| the Native, Métis, free Black, Irish and German town the register implies | T-1177 |
| the transient population: land-sale visitors, the harbour gang, crews ashore | T-1178 |
| the stores, shops, professions and lodging houses with no proprietor named | T-1184 to T-1188 |
